  543      /// Execute a tool by name
  544      pub async fn execute(&self, name: &str, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  545          let tools = self.tools.read().await;
  546          let resolved_name = Self::resolve_tool_name(name);
  547          if let Some(policy) = session_tool_policy(&ctx.session_id) {
  548              if let Some(allowed) = policy.allowed_tools.as_ref()
  549                  && !allowed.contains(resolved_name)
  550              {
  551                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is not allowed", resolved_name));
  552              }
  553              if policy.disabled_tools.contains(resolved_name) {
  554                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is disabled", resolved_name));
  555              }
  556          }
  557          let tool = match tools.get(resolved_name) {
  558              Some(tool) => tool.clone(),
  559              None => {
  560                  // List available tools so the model can recover instead of
  561                  // spiraling through hallucinated names like "ToolSearch" (#104).
  562                  let mut available: Vec<&str> = tools.keys().map(|k| k.as_str()).collect();
  563                  available.sort_unstable();
  564                  let suggestions = Self::closest_tool_names(name, &available);
  565                  let mut msg = format!("Unknown tool: {name}.");
  566                  if !suggestions.is_empty() {
  567                      msg.push_str(&format!(" Did you mean: {}?", suggestions.join(", ")));
  568                  }
  569                  msg.push_str(&format!(" Available tools: {}.", available.join(", ")));
  570                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(msg));
  571              }
  572          };
  573
  574          // Drop the lock before executing
  575          drop(tools);
  576
  577          // User-configured pre_tool gate: external policy hook that can block
  578          // this call (exit 2). Skipped entirely when not configured.
  579          if crate::hooks::hook_configured("pre_tool") {
  580              let input_json = input.to_string();
  581              let working_dir = ctx
  582                  .working_dir
  583                  .as_ref()
  584                  .map(|dir| dir.display().to_string());
  585              let decision = crate::hooks::run_pre_tool_gate(
  586                  &ctx.session_id,
  587                  working_dir.as_deref(),
  588                  resolved_name,
  589                  &input_json,
  590              )
  591              .await;
  592              if let crate::hooks::GateDecision::Block { reason } = decision {
  593                  let mut fields =
  594                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("blocked", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  595                  fields.push(("block_reason".to_string(), reason.clone()));
  596                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  597                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
  598                      "Tool call blocked by pre_tool hook: {reason}"
  599                  ));
  600              }
  601          }
  602
  603          crate::logging::event_info(
  604              "TOOL_LIFECYCLE",
  605              Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("start", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx),
  606          );
  607
  608          let started_at = std::time::Instant::now();
  609          let result = tool.execute(input.clone(), ctx.clone()).await;
  610          let latency_ms = started_at.elapsed().as_millis().min(u128::from(u64::MAX)) as u64;
  611
  612          crate::telemetry::record_tool_execution(resolved_name, &input, result.is_ok(), latency_ms);
  613          Self::fire_post_tool_hook(resolved_name, &ctx, &result, latency_ms);
  614
  615          let mut output = match result {
  616              Ok(output) => output,
  617              Err(error) => {
  618                  let mut fields =
  619                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("error", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  620                  fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  621                  fields.push(("error".to_string(), crate::util::format_error_chain(&error)));
  622                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  623                  return Err(error);
  624              }
  625          };
  626
  627          // Context overflow guard: check if this output would push us over the limit
  628          output = self.guard_context_overflow(name, output).await;
  629
  630          let mut fields = Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("done", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  631          fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  632          fields.push(("output_bytes".to_string(), output.output.len().to_string()));
  633          fields.push((
  634              "output_chars".to_string(),
  635              output.output.chars().count().to_string(),
  636          ));
  637          fields.push(("image_count".to_string(), output.images.len().to_string()));
  638          crate::logging::event_info("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  639
  640          Ok(output)
  641      }
  642
  643      /// Check if a tool output would overflow the context window and truncate if needed.
  644      /// Returns the (possibly truncated) output.
  645      async fn guard_context_overflow(&self, tool_name: &str, output: ToolOutput) -> ToolOutput {
  646          let compaction = self.compaction.read().await;
  647          let budget = compaction.token_budget();
  648          if budget == 0 {
  649              return output;
  650          }
  651
  652          let current_tokens = compaction.effective_token_count();
  653          let output_tokens = Self::estimate_tokens(&output.output);
  654
  655          // Check 1: Would adding this output push us over the safety threshold?
  656          let projected = current_tokens + output_tokens;
  657          let threshold_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::CONTEXT_GUARD_THRESHOLD) as usize;
  658
  659          // Check 2: Is this single output unreasonably large relative to budget?
  660          let single_max_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_FRACTION) as usize;
  661
  662          let needs_truncation = projected > threshold_tokens || output_tokens > single_max_tokens;
  663
  664          if !needs_truncation {
  665              return output;
  666          }
  667
  668          // Calculate how many tokens we can afford for this output
  669          let remaining = if current_tokens < threshold_tokens {
  670              threshold_tokens - current_tokens
  671          } else {
  672              // Already over threshold — allow a small amount for the error message

  551      /// Maximum fraction of context budget a single tool output may consume.
  552      /// Outputs that would push total context beyond this are truncated.
  553      const CONTEXT_GUARD_THRESHOLD: f32 = 0.90;
  554
  555      /// Fire the `post_tool` observer hook with tool outcome metadata.
  556      /// No-op (without building the payload) when the hook is not configured.
  557      fn fire_post_tool_hook(
  558          resolved_name: &str,
  559          ctx: &ToolContext,
  560          result: &Result<ToolOutput>,
  561          latency_ms: u64,
  562      ) {
  563          if !crate::hooks::hook_configured("post_tool") {
  564              return;
  565          }
  566          let mut event = crate::hooks::HookEvent::new("post_tool")
  567              .session_id(ctx.session_id.clone())
  568              .field("TOOL_NAME", resolved_name)
  569              .field("STATUS", if result.is_ok() { "ok" } else { "error" })
  570              .field("DURATION_MS", latency_ms.to_string());
  571          if let Some(dir) = &ctx.working_dir {
  572              event = event.cwd(dir.display().to_string());
  573          }
  574          match result {
  575              Ok(output) => {
  576                  event = event.field("OUTPUT_BYTES", output.output.len().to_string());
  577              }
  578              Err(error) => {
  579                  const ERROR_LIMIT: usize = 1000;
  580                  let message: String = error.to_string().chars().take(ERROR_LIMIT).collect();
  581                  event = event.field("ERROR", message);
  582              }
  583          }
  584          crate::hooks::dispatch_observer(event);
  585      }
  586
  587      /// Maximum fraction of context budget a single tool output may occupy.
  588      /// Even if we have room, a single output shouldn't dominate the context.
  589      const SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_FRACTION: f32 = 0.30;
  590
  591      /// Hard ceiling on a single tool output, independent of context budget.
  592      ///
  593      /// A fraction alone is not enough. On a model reporting a 1M-token window,
  594      /// 30% permits a 300k-token single result, so a repo-wide grep sailed
  595      /// through the guard and cost 233k tokens in one call. No individual tool
  596      /// result is worth that much of any window: past roughly 50k tokens the
  597      /// caller is reading a haystack, not an answer, and should narrow the query.
  598      /// The effective ceiling is the smaller of this and the budget fraction, so
  599      /// small windows still get proportional protection.
  600      const SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_TOKENS: usize = 50_000;
  601
  602      /// Message returned instead of an oversized tool result.
  603      ///
  604      /// It has one job: make the price legible and the retry obvious. The caller
  605      /// gets the exact cost, what would survive truncation, the cheap fixes, and
  606      /// the exact flag to pass if they really want the whole thing.
  607      fn oversized_output_refusal(
  608          output_tokens: usize,
  609          affordable_tokens: usize,
  610          current_tokens: usize,
  611          budget: usize,
  612      ) -> String {
  613          let percent_of_budget = if budget > 0 {
  614              (output_tokens as f32 / budget as f32) * 100.0
  615          } else {
  616              0.0
  617          };
  618          format!(
  619              "⚠️ OUTPUT WITHHELD: this result is ~{output}k tokens ({percent:.0}% of the \
  620               {budget}k context budget, of which {used}k is already used), so it was not \
  621               returned. Nothing was added to the context except this message.\n\n\
  622               Narrow the request first: add or tighten `path`, `glob`, or `type`, set \
  623               `max_files`/`max_regions`, use `paths_only` when you only need locations, or \
  624               read a specific line range. A targeted query is almost always the better \
  625               answer than a truncated dump.\n\n\
  626               If you genuinely need this output and accept the token cost, repeat the same \
  627               call with `\"accept_large_output\": true`. That returns the first ~{affordable}k \
  628               tokens and permanently spends them from this session's context.",
  629              output = output_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  630              percent = percent_of_budget,
  631              budget = budget / 1000,
  632              used = current_tokens / 1000,
  633              affordable = affordable_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  634          )
  635      }
  636
  637      /// Execute a tool by name
  638      pub async fn execute(&self, name: &str, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  639          // Mark this call in-flight for the whole execution so the missing
  640          // tool-output repair paths do not mistake a slow tool for an
  641          // interrupted one and inject a duplicate synthetic result. See
  642          // `tool::inflight`.
  643          let _in_flight = inflight::mark_tool_in_flight(&ctx.tool_call_id);
  644          let tools = self.tools.read().await;
  645          let resolved_name = Self::resolve_tool_name(name);
  646          if let Some(policy) = session_tool_policy(&ctx.session_id) {
  647              if let Some(allowed) = policy.allowed_tools.as_ref()
  648                  && !tool_name_is_allowed(allowed, resolved_name)
  649              {
  650                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is not allowed", resolved_name));
  651              }
  652              if tool_name_is_disabled(&policy.disabled_tools, resolved_name) {
  653                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is disabled", resolved_name));
  654              }
  655          }
  656          let tool = match tools.get(resolved_name) {
  657              Some(tool) => tool.clone(),
  658              None => {
  659                  // List available tools so the model can recover instead of
  660                  // spiraling through hallucinated names like "ToolSearch" (#104).
  661                  let mut available: Vec<&str> = tools.keys().map(|k| k.as_str()).collect();
  662                  available.sort_unstable();
  663                  let suggestions = Self::closest_tool_names(name, &available);
  664                  let mut msg = format!("Unknown tool: {name}.");
  665                  if !suggestions.is_empty() {
  666                      msg.push_str(&format!(" Did you mean: {}?", suggestions.join(", ")));
  667                  }
  668                  msg.push_str(&format!(" Available tools: {}.", available.join(", ")));
  669                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(msg));
  670              }
  671          };

02
L1factsecurity

02 · Bash 有确定性危险命令 gate，灾难目标直接拒绝

源码事实

所有前后台 Bash 在 dispatch 前调用 jcode_command_risk::assess/gate；普通危险命令要求模型带针对已拒绝调用的 justification 反思后再发，根目录、HOME、凭证库、设备节点等 catastrophic targets 直接 deny。

白话解释

不是让模型自己判断 rm 是否危险：命令先过代码规则；有些可解释后重试，有些目标永远不给过。

对自研 Harness 的含义

比纯提示词可靠，但命令解析器仍需要覆盖 shell 展开、别名、子 shell 和平台差异。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 1–19
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 20–39
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

crates/jcode-app-core/src/tool/bash_destructive_gate.rs:1–39固定提交 ↗
    1  //! The destructive-command gate for the `bash` tool (issue #604).
    2  //!
    3  //! Kept in its own file so the policy seam is easy to find and review: this is
    4  //! the only thing standing between a model's `rm -rf` and the user's data.
    5
    6  /// Apply the deterministic destructive-command gate, returning refusal text
    7  /// when the command must not run as-issued.
    8  ///
    9  /// Stage 1 is a pure blast-radius assessment; stage 2 turns a `Confirm` verdict
   10  /// into a reflection prompt that a blind retry cannot satisfy. Catastrophic
   11  /// targets (`/`, `$HOME`, credential stores, device nodes) are denied outright.
   12  /// See issue #604.
   13  pub(super) fn destructive_command_refusal(
   14      command: &str,
   15      justification: Option<&str>,
   16      working_dir: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
   17  ) -> Option<String> {
   18      let risk_ctx = jcode_command_risk::RiskContext::from_env(working_dir);
   19      let assessment = jcode_command_risk::assess(command, &risk_ctx);
   20      if assessment.level.runs_immediately() {
   21          return None;
   22      }
   23
   24      let justification = jcode_command_risk::Justification {
   25          text: justification.map(str::to_string),
   26      };
   27      match jcode_command_risk::gate(&assessment, &justification) {
   28          jcode_command_risk::GateOutcome::Allow => None,
   29          jcode_command_risk::GateOutcome::Deny { reason } => {
   30              crate::logging::warn(&format!("[bash] denied destructive command: {command}"));
   31              Some(reason)
   32          }
   33          jcode_command_risk::GateOutcome::Reflect { prompt } => {
   34              crate::logging::info(&format!(
   35                  "[bash] destructive command held for justification: {command}"
   36              ));
   37              Some(prompt)
   38          }
   39      }
   40  }
   41
   42  /// The `bash` tool's JSON schema, including the `justification` field the
   43  /// destructive-command gate consumes.
   44  ///
   45  /// Lives beside the gate so the schema and the policy that reads it stay in
   46  /// sync, and so bash.rs stays inside the code-size budget.
   47  pub(super) fn bash_parameters_schema() -> serde_json::Value {
   48      let cmd_desc = if cfg!(windows) {
   49          "The Windows command to execute via cmd.exe. Use cmd.exe syntax and quoting, not Bash syntax. Double-quote individual arguments containing spaces. Invoke PowerShell explicitly when PowerShell syntax is needed. If you write a long-running script or loop for run_in_background=true, make it print progress lines. Preferred format: `JCODE_PROGRESS {json}`."
   50      } else {
   51          "The bash command to execute. If you write a long-running script or loop for run_in_background=true, make it print progress lines. Preferred format: `JCODE_PROGRESS {json}`. Put large temporary files and worktrees under `$JCODE_SCRATCH_DIR`, not `/tmp`, because `/tmp` may be RAM-backed."
   52      };
   53      serde_json::json!({
   54          "type": "object",
   55          "required": ["command"],
   56          "properties": {
   57              "intent": crate::tool::intent_schema_property(),
   58              "command": {
   59                  "type": "string",
   60                  "description": cmd_desc
   61              },
   62              "timeout": {
   63                  "type": "integer",
   64                  "description": "Timeout in MILLISECONDS (not seconds). Kills the command when exceeded and reports exit 124. e.g. 1000 = 1s, 600000 = 10min. Omit to run with no timeout; do NOT pass small values like 1000 for long jobs such as builds or test suites."
   65              },
   66              "run_in_background": {
   67                  "type": "boolean",
   68                  "description": format!("Run in background. {}", super::BACKGROUND_PROGRESS_GUIDANCE)
   69              },
   70              "notify": {
   71                  "type": "boolean",
   72                  "description": "Notify on completion."
   73              },
   74              "wake": {
   75                  "type": "boolean",
   76                  "description": "Wake on completion."
   77              },
   78              "justification": {
   79                  "type": "string",
   80                  "description": "Only for re-issuing a command the destructive-command gate refused. Explain which specific user request this command serves. Do not set it preemptively."
   81              }
   82          }
   83      })
   84  }

03
L1limitationsecurity

03 · 默认执行环境不是 OS 级沙箱

源码事实

Bash 直接用 bash/cmd.exe 子进程并继承宿主环境，在 session working_dir 下运行；代码提供 kill_on_drop、process group、timeout、策略 gate 与 hook，但审计范围内未见 bubblewrap、Seatbelt、容器或 restricted token 作为默认执行层。

白话解释

它有门禁和刹车，但不是把命令关进另一个房间；一旦获准，进程仍在你的机器权限下跑。

对自研 Harness 的含义

企业部署应外包给容器/VM/OS sandbox 或增加 capability-based executor，不能把危险命令 gate 等同隔离。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 724–741
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 742–760
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

边界 / 风险
  • 这是对所审提交的内建 Bash 执行路径结论；用户可在 jcode 外层自行运行容器或受限账户。

  730      async fn execute_foreground(
  731          &self,
  732          params: &BashInput,
  733          ctx: &ToolContext,
  734      ) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  735          #[cfg(unix)]
  736          if self.supports_reload_persistence(ctx) {
  737              return self
  738                  .execute_reload_persistable_foreground(params, ctx)
  739                  .await;
  740          }
  741
  742          let timeout_ms = params.timeout.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS).min(600000);
  743          let timeout_duration = Duration::from_millis(timeout_ms);
  744
  745          let has_stdin_channel = ctx.stdin_request_tx.is_some();
  746
  747          let mut command = build_shell_command(&params.command);
  748          command
  749              .kill_on_drop(true)
  750              .stdout(Stdio::piped())
  751              .stderr(Stdio::piped());
  752
  753          if has_stdin_channel {
  754              command.stdin(Stdio::piped());
  755          }
  756
  757          if let Some(ref dir) = ctx.working_dir {
  758              command.current_dir(dir);
  759          }
  760          let mut child = command.spawn()?;
  761
  762          let child_pid = child.id().unwrap_or(0);
  763          let stdin_handle = child.stdin.take();
  764          let stdout_handle = child.stdout.take();
  765          let stderr_handle = child.stderr.take();
  766
  767          // Owned copies so the work can outlive this call if it is promoted to a
  768          // background task on timeout.
  769          let title = params
  770              .intent
  771              .clone()
  772              .unwrap_or_else(|| params.command.clone());
  773          let stdin_tx = ctx.stdin_request_tx.clone();
  774          let tool_call_id = ctx.tool_call_id.clone();
  775          let title_for_work = title.clone();
  776
  777          // Run the command (read stdout/stderr, service stdin, wait for exit) in a
  778          // dedicated task so that, if it exceeds the foreground timeout, we can hand
  779          // the still-running task off to the background manager instead of killing it.
  780          let mut work_handle: tokio::task::JoinHandle<Result<ToolOutput>> =
  781              tokio::spawn(async move {
  782                  let stdout_task = tokio::spawn(async move {
  783                      let mut buf = String::new();
  784                      if let Some(mut out) = stdout_handle {
  785                          let _ = out.read_to_string(&mut buf).await;
  786                      }
  787                      buf
  788                  });
  789
  790                  let stderr_task = tokio::spawn(async move {
  791                      let mut buf = String::new();
  792                      if let Some(mut err) = stderr_handle {
  793                          let _ = err.read_to_string(&mut buf).await;
  794                      }
  795                      buf
  796                  });
  797
  798                  let stdin_task = if has_stdin_channel {
  799                      Some(tokio::spawn(async move {
  800                          if let (Some(mut stdin_pipe), Some(stdin_tx)) = (stdin_handle, stdin_tx) {
  801                              tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(STDIN_INITIAL_DELAY_MS)).await;
  802
  803                              let mut request_counter = 0u32;
  804                              loop {
  805                                  #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
  806                                  let state = stdin_detect::linux::check_process_tree(child_pid);
  807                                  #[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
  808                                  let state = stdin_detect::is_waiting_for_stdin(child_pid);
  809
  810                                  if state == StdinState::Reading {
  811                                      request_counter += 1;
  812                                      let request_id =
  813                                          format!("stdin-{}-{}", tool_call_id, request_counter);
  814                                      let (response_tx, response_rx) =
  815                                          tokio::sync::oneshot::channel();
  816
  817                                      let request = StdinInputRequest {
  818                                          request_id,
  819                                          prompt: String::new(),
  820                                          is_password: false,
  821                                          response_tx,
  822                                      };
  823
  824                                      if stdin_tx.send(request).is_err() {
  825                                          break;
  826                                      }
  827
  828                                      match response_rx.await {
  829                                          Ok(input) => {
  830                                              let line = if input.ends_with('\n') {
  831                                                  input

  724
  725      fn parameters_schema(&self) -> Value {
  726          destructive_gate::bash_parameters_schema()
  727      }
  728
  729      async fn execute(&self, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  730          let mut params: BashInput = serde_json::from_value(input)?;
  731          let run_in_background = params.run_in_background.unwrap_or(false);
  732
  733          // Destructive-command gate (#604), before background dispatch.
  734          if let Some(refusal) = destructive_command_refusal(
  735              &params.command,
  736              params.justification.as_deref(),
  737              ctx.working_dir.clone(),
  738          ) {
  739              return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(refusal));
  740          }
  741
  742          #[cfg(unix)]
  743          if let Some(wrapped) = wrap_repo_cargo_commands(&params.command, ctx.working_dir.as_deref())
  744          {
  745              params.command = wrapped;
  746          }
  747
  748          if run_in_background {
  749              return self.execute_background(params, ctx).await;
  750          }
  751
  752          // Auto-detect browser bridge commands and rewrite them to the installed
  753          // binary when available, but do not run setup automatically. Browser
  754          // setup should stay an explicit status/setup flow rather than a default
  755          // side effect of trying to use the browser.
  756          if crate::browser::is_browser_command(&params.command) {
  757              params.command = crate::browser::rewrite_command_with_full_path(&params.command);
  758
  759              // Start/attach a browser session for this jcode session.
  760              // This gives each agent its own browser tab, preventing
  761              // multi-agent conflicts when using the browser bridge.
  762              if !cfg!(windows)
  763                  && std::env::var("BROWSER_SESSION").is_err()
  764                  && let Some(session_name) = crate::browser::ensure_browser_session(&ctx.session_id)
  765              {
  766                  params.command = format!("BROWSER_SESSION={} {}", session_name, params.command);
  767              }
  768          }
  769
  770          // Foreground execution with stdin detection
  771          self.execute_foreground(&params, &ctx).await
  772      }
  773  }
  774
  775  impl BashTool {
  776      async fn execute_foreground(
  777          &self,
  778          params: &BashInput,
  779          ctx: &ToolContext,
  780      ) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  781          #[cfg(unix)]
  782          if self.supports_reload_persistence(ctx) {
  783              return self
  784                  .execute_reload_persistable_foreground(params, ctx)
  785                  .await;
  786          }
  787
  788          let timeout_ms = params.timeout.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS).min(600000);
  789          let timeout_duration = Duration::from_millis(timeout_ms);
  790
  791          let has_stdin_channel = ctx.stdin_request_tx.is_some();
  792
  793          let mut command = build_shell_command(&params.command);
  794          command
  795              .kill_on_drop(true)
  796              .stdout(Stdio::piped())
  797              .stderr(Stdio::piped());
  798
  799          if has_stdin_channel {
  800              command.stdin(Stdio::piped());
  801          }
  802
  803          if let Some(ref dir) = ctx.working_dir {
  804              command.current_dir(dir);
  805          }
  806          let mut child = command.spawn()?;
  807
  808          let child_pid = child.id().unwrap_or(0);
  809          let stdin_handle = child.stdin.take();
  810          let stdout_handle = child.stdout.take();
  811          let stderr_handle = child.stderr.take();
  812
  813          // Owned copies so the work can outlive this call if it is promoted to a
  814          // background task on timeout.
  815          let title = params
  816              .intent
  817              .clone()
  818              .unwrap_or_else(|| params.command.clone());
  819          let stdin_tx = ctx.stdin_request_tx.clone();
  820          let tool_call_id = ctx.tool_call_id.clone();
  821          let title_for_work = title.clone();
  822
  823          // Run the command (read stdout/stderr, service stdin, wait for exit) in a
  824          // dedicated task so that, if it exceeds the foreground timeout, we can hand
  825          // the still-running task off to the background manager instead of killing it.
  826          let mut work_handle: tokio::task::JoinHandle<Result<ToolOutput>> =
  827              tokio::spawn(async move {
  828                  let stdout_task = tokio::spawn(async move {
  829                      let mut buf = String::new();
  830                      if let Some(mut out) = stdout_handle {
  831                          let _ = out.read_to_string(&mut buf).await;
08
DIMENSION · OBSERVABILITY

观测、日志与回放

4 条可定位结论；每条按事实 → 白话 → 工程影响 → 源码摘录展开。

01
L1factobservability

01 · Session 用完整 snapshot 加 append-only JSONL journal

源码事实

save 比较 metadata/vector dirty state：需要全量时 checkpoint snapshot 并删 journal，否则只追加 messages/env snapshots/memory injections/replay events delta；journal 超上限或 append 失败再回退 snapshot。

白话解释

平时只往流水账追加新变化，偶尔把整本账重抄成快照；这样频繁保存不会每次重写全部历史。

对自研 Harness 的含义

兼顾耐久与 I/O；多写者必须依靠更上层会话所有权避免交错 append。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 307–350
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 351–395
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

02
L1factobservability

02 · 损坏 journal 跳过坏行、挽救 glued entries，并在下次保存自愈

源码事实

replay 不在首个 parse error 停止，而是继续后续行；对 torn append 与下一条粘在同一行的情况扫描结构起点并 stream-parse。发现腐坏后备份 corrupt.jsonl，强制下次 checkpoint 把已挽救状态固化。

白话解释

流水账中间坏一行，不会把后面所有对话一起判死；能捞的条目先捞出来，再重写一本干净快照。

对自研 Harness 的含义

长会话恢复质量高，且保留法证副本。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 26–76
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 77–128
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

叙述来自历史证据账本 · 119301ac9d60 · 打开账本提交 ↗
crates/jcode-base/src/session/persistence.rs:26–128固定提交 ↗
   26  /// Attempt to recover complete entries from a journal line that failed the
   27  /// strict one-entry-per-line parse.
   28  ///
   29  /// If a writer died mid-append (torn line without a trailing newline), the
   30  /// next successful append starts writing on the same line, producing
   31  /// `<torn json><complete entry json>\n` or `<entry json><entry json>\n`.
   32  /// Serialized entries always begin with `{"meta":` (struct field order), so
   33  /// scan for candidate starts and stream-parse consecutive complete entries
   34  /// from the first position that yields any.
   35  fn salvage_glued_journal_entries(line: &str, mut apply: impl FnMut(SessionJournalEntry)) -> usize {
   36      const ENTRY_START: &str = "{\"meta\":";
   37      let mut salvaged = 0usize;
   38      let mut search_from = 0usize;
   39      while let Some(rel) = line
   40          .get(search_from..)
   41          .and_then(|rest| rest.find(ENTRY_START))
   42      {
   43          let candidate_start = search_from + rel;
   44          let mut stream = serde_json::Deserializer::from_str(&line[candidate_start..])
   45              .into_iter::<SessionJournalEntry>();
   46          let mut parsed = Vec::new();
   47          for item in &mut stream {
   48              match item {
   49                  Ok(entry) => parsed.push(entry),
   50                  Err(_) => break,
   51              }
   52          }
   53          if !parsed.is_empty() {
   54              salvaged += parsed.len();
   55              for entry in parsed {
   56                  apply(entry);
   57              }
   58              break;
   59          }
   60          search_from = candidate_start + ENTRY_START.len();
   61      }
   62      salvaged
   63  }
   64
   65  /// Replay every parseable entry from a session journal, tolerating corrupt
   66  /// lines instead of truncating the transcript at the first bad byte.
   67  ///
   68  /// Journals are append-only JSONL written by `append_json_line_fast`. A crash,
   69  /// full disk, or (historically) interleaved multi-write appends can leave a
   70  /// torn or glued line behind. The old replay loop stopped at the first parse
   71  /// failure, silently dropping every later entry, which surfaced as "my last
   72  /// prompt is missing" after resuming a long session. Skipping only the bad
   73  /// line preserves the rest of the transcript; per-entry `meta` snapshots make
   74  /// later entries self-sufficient for metadata, and appended messages after a
   75  /// gap are far better than losing the whole tail.
   76  fn replay_journal_lines(
   77      journal_path: &Path,
   78      mut apply: impl FnMut(SessionJournalEntry),
   79  ) -> Result<JournalReplayStats> {
   80      let mut stats = JournalReplayStats::default();
   81      if !journal_path.exists() {
   82          return Ok(stats);
   83      }
   84
   85      let file = std::fs::File::open(journal_path)?;
   86      let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   87      for (line_idx, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
   88          let line = line?;
   89          let trimmed = line.trim();
   90          if trimmed.is_empty() {
   91              continue;
   92          }
   93          match serde_json::from_str::<SessionJournalEntry>(trimmed) {
   94              Ok(entry) => {
   95                  stats.entries += 1;
   96                  apply(entry);
   97              }
   98              Err(err) => {
   99                  stats.skipped_lines += 1;
  100                  let salvaged = salvage_glued_journal_entries(trimmed, &mut apply);
  101                  stats.entries += salvaged;
  102                  stats.salvaged_entries += salvaged;
  103                  crate::logging::warn(&format!(
  104                      "Session journal parse failed at {} line {} ({}); salvaged {} glued entr{} and continuing replay",
  105                      journal_path.display(),
  106                      line_idx + 1,
  107                      err,
  108                      salvaged,
  109                      if salvaged == 1 { "y" } else { "ies" }
  110                  ));
  111              }
  112          }
  113      }
  114
  115      if stats.is_corrupt() {
  116          crate::logging::event_warn(
  117              "SESSION_PERSISTENCE",
  118              vec![
  119                  ("phase", "journal_replay_corruption".to_string()),
  120                  ("path", journal_path.display().to_string()),
  121                  ("entries_replayed", stats.entries.to_string()),
  122                  ("lines_skipped", stats.skipped_lines.to_string()),
  123                  ("entries_salvaged", stats.salvaged_entries.to_string()),
  124              ],
  125          );
  126      }
  127
  128      Ok(stats)
  129  }
  130
  131  impl Session {
  132      fn apply_journal_entry(&mut self, entry: SessionJournalEntry) {
  133          self.apply_journal_meta(entry.meta);
  134          self.messages.extend(entry.append_messages);
  135          self.env_snapshots.extend(entry.append_env_snapshots);
  136          self.memory_injections
  137              .extend(entry.append_memory_injections);
  138          self.replay_events.extend(entry.append_replay_events);
  139          self.mark_memory_profile_dirty();
  140      }
  141
  142      fn checkpoint_snapshot(&mut self, snapshot_path: &Path, journal_path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
  143          storage::write_json_fast(snapshot_path, self)?;
  144          if journal_path.exists() {
  145              let _ = std::fs::remove_file(journal_path);
  146          }
  147          self.reset_persist_state(true);
  148          Ok(())
  149      }
  150
  151      /// After replaying a journal that contained unparseable lines, force the
  152      /// next `save()` to checkpoint a full snapshot (which deletes the corrupt
  153      /// journal) so the salvaged in-memory state becomes durable and the bad
  154      /// lines can never be replayed again. A best-effort copy of the corrupt
  155      /// journal is kept next to it for forensics.
  156      fn schedule_checkpoint_after_corrupt_journal(&mut self, journal_path: &Path) {
  157          self.mark_messages_full_dirty();
  158          let backup_path = journal_path.with_extension("corrupt.jsonl");
  159          if let Err(err) = std::fs::copy(journal_path, &backup_path) {
  160              crate::logging::warn(&format!(
  161                  "Failed to back up corrupt session journal {} to {}: {}",
  162                  journal_path.display(),
  163                  backup_path.display(),
  164                  err
  165              ));
  166          }
  167          crate::logging::warn(&format!(
  168              "Session {} journal {} contained corrupt lines; next save will checkpoint a full snapshot (backup at {})",
  169              self.id,
  170              journal_path.display(),
  171              backup_path.display()
  172          ));
  173      }
  174
  175      pub fn load_from_path(path: &Path) -> Result<Self> {
  176          let load_start = Instant::now();
  177          let snapshot_bytes = file_len_or_zero(path);
  178          let snapshot_start = Instant::now();
  179          let mut session: Session = storage::read_json(path)?;
  180          let snapshot_ms = snapshot_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  181          let journal_path = session_journal_path_from_snapshot(path);
  182          let journal_bytes = file_len_or_zero(&journal_path);
  183          let journal_start = Instant::now();
  184          let replay_stats = replay_journal_lines(&journal_path, |entry| {
  185              session.apply_journal_entry(entry);
  186          })?;
  187          let journal_entries = replay_stats.entries;
  188          let journal_ms = journal_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  189          let finalize_start = Instant::now();
  190          session.reset_persist_state(path.exists());
  191          session.reset_provider_messages_cache();
  192          session.mark_memory_profile_dirty();
  193          if replay_stats.is_corrupt() {
  194              session.schedule_checkpoint_after_corrupt_journal(&journal_path);
  195          }
  196          let finalize_ms = finalize_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  197          crate::logging::info(&format!(
  198              "[TIMING] session_load: session={}, snapshot={}ms, journal={}ms, finalize={}ms, snapshot_bytes={}, journal_bytes={}, journal_entries={}, messages={}, env_snapshots={}, replay_events={}, total={}ms",
  199              session.id,

crates/jcode-base/src/session/persistence.rs · 71fa60c4dc87当前源码 ↗
   26  /// Attempt to recover complete entries from a journal line that failed the
   27  /// strict one-entry-per-line parse.
   28  ///
   29  /// If a writer died mid-append (torn line without a trailing newline), the
   30  /// next successful append starts writing on the same line, producing
   31  /// `<torn json><complete entry json>\n` or `<entry json><entry json>\n`.
   32  /// Serialized entries always begin with `{"meta":` (struct field order), so
   33  /// scan for candidate starts and stream-parse consecutive complete entries
   34  /// from the first position that yields any.
   35  fn salvage_glued_journal_entries(line: &str, mut apply: impl FnMut(SessionJournalEntry)) -> usize {
   36      const ENTRY_START: &str = "{\"meta\":";
   37      let mut salvaged = 0usize;
   38      let mut search_from = 0usize;
   39      while let Some(rel) = line
   40          .get(search_from..)
   41          .and_then(|rest| rest.find(ENTRY_START))
   42      {
   43          let candidate_start = search_from + rel;
   44          let mut stream = serde_json::Deserializer::from_str(&line[candidate_start..])
   45              .into_iter::<SessionJournalEntry>();
   46          let mut parsed = Vec::new();
   47          for item in &mut stream {
   48              match item {
   49                  Ok(entry) => parsed.push(entry),
   50                  Err(_) => break,
   51              }
   52          }
   53          if !parsed.is_empty() {
   54              salvaged += parsed.len();
   55              for entry in parsed {
   56                  apply(entry);
   57              }
   58              break;
   59          }
   60          search_from = candidate_start + ENTRY_START.len();
   61      }
   62      salvaged
   63  }
   64
   65  /// Replay every parseable entry from a session journal, tolerating corrupt
   66  /// lines instead of truncating the transcript at the first bad byte.
   67  ///
   68  /// Journals are append-only JSONL written by `append_json_line_fast`. A crash,
   69  /// full disk, or (historically) interleaved multi-write appends can leave a
   70  /// torn or glued line behind. The old replay loop stopped at the first parse
   71  /// failure, silently dropping every later entry, which surfaced as "my last
   72  /// prompt is missing" after resuming a long session. Skipping only the bad
   73  /// line preserves the rest of the transcript; per-entry `meta` snapshots make
   74  /// later entries self-sufficient for metadata, and appended messages after a
   75  /// gap are far better than losing the whole tail.
   76  fn replay_journal_lines(
   77      journal_path: &Path,
   78      mut apply: impl FnMut(SessionJournalEntry),
   79  ) -> Result<JournalReplayStats> {
   80      let mut stats = JournalReplayStats::default();
   81      if !journal_path.exists() {
   82          return Ok(stats);
   83      }
   84
   85      let file = std::fs::File::open(journal_path)?;
   86      let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   87      for (line_idx, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
   88          let line = line?;
   89          let trimmed = line.trim();
   90          if trimmed.is_empty() {
   91              continue;
   92          }
   93          match serde_json::from_str::<SessionJournalEntry>(trimmed) {
   94              Ok(entry) => {
   95                  stats.entries += 1;
   96                  apply(entry);
   97              }
   98              Err(err) => {
   99                  stats.skipped_lines += 1;
  100                  let salvaged = salvage_glued_journal_entries(trimmed, &mut apply);
  101                  stats.entries += salvaged;
  102                  stats.salvaged_entries += salvaged;
  103                  crate::logging::warn(&format!(
  104                      "Session journal parse failed at {} line {} ({}); salvaged {} glued entr{} and continuing replay",
  105                      journal_path.display(),
  106                      line_idx + 1,
  107                      err,
  108                      salvaged,
  109                      if salvaged == 1 { "y" } else { "ies" }
  110                  ));
  111              }
  112          }
  113      }
  114
  115      if stats.is_corrupt() {
  116          crate::logging::event_warn(
  117              "SESSION_PERSISTENCE",
  118              vec![
  119                  ("phase", "journal_replay_corruption".to_string()),
  120                  ("path", journal_path.display().to_string()),
  121                  ("entries_replayed", stats.entries.to_string()),
  122                  ("lines_skipped", stats.skipped_lines.to_string()),
  123                  ("entries_salvaged", stats.salvaged_entries.to_string()),
  124              ],
  125          );
  126      }
  127
  128      Ok(stats)
  129  }
  130
  131  impl Session {
  132      fn pre_wipe_backup_path(path: &Path, timestamp: i64) -> PathBuf {
  133          let file_name = path
  134              .file_name()
  135              .map(|name| name.to_string_lossy())
  136              .unwrap_or_default();
  137          path.with_file_name(format!("{file_name}.pre-wipe-{timestamp}.bak"))
  138      }
  139
  140      /// Preserve the last durable transcript before an empty in-memory session
  141      /// can replace it. This is deliberately best-effort: the hard rejection in
  142      /// `checkpoint_snapshot` is what prevents data loss, while these copies make
  143      /// recovery possible if another caller ever bypasses that invariant.
  144      fn guard_snapshot_shrink(&self, snapshot_path: &Path, journal_path: &Path) {
  145          const MIN_TRANSCRIPT_SNAPSHOT_BYTES: u64 = 4 * 1024;
  146
  147          if !self.messages.is_empty()
  148              || file_len_or_zero(snapshot_path) <= MIN_TRANSCRIPT_SNAPSHOT_BYTES
  149          {
  150              return;
  151          }
  152
  153          let timestamp = Utc::now().timestamp_millis();
  154          let snapshot_backup = Self::pre_wipe_backup_path(snapshot_path, timestamp);
  155          let journal_backup = Self::pre_wipe_backup_path(journal_path, timestamp);
  156          let mut backup_errors = Vec::new();
  157
  158          if let Err(err) = std::fs::copy(snapshot_path, &snapshot_backup) {
  159              backup_errors.push(format!("snapshot {}: {err}", snapshot_backup.display()));
  160          }
  161          if journal_path.exists()
  162              && let Err(err) = std::fs::copy(journal_path, &journal_backup)
  163          {
  164              backup_errors.push(format!("journal {}: {err}", journal_backup.display()));
  165          }
  166
  167          let fields = vec![
  168              ("phase", "pre_wipe_backup".to_string()),
  169              ("session_id", self.id.clone()),
  170              ("snapshot_path", snapshot_path.display().to_string()),
  171              ("snapshot_backup", snapshot_backup.display().to_string()),
  172              ("journal_backup", journal_backup.display().to_string()),
  173              ("errors", backup_errors.join("; ")),
  174          ];
  175          crate::logging::event_warn("SESSION_PERSISTENCE", fields);
  176      }
  177
  178      fn apply_journal_entry(&mut self, entry: SessionJournalEntry) {
  179          self.apply_journal_meta(entry.meta);
  180          self.messages.extend(entry.append_messages);
  181          self.env_snapshots.extend(entry.append_env_snapshots);
  182          self.memory_injections
  183              .extend(entry.append_memory_injections);
  184          self.replay_events.extend(entry.append_replay_events);
  185          self.mark_memory_profile_dirty();
  186      }
  187
  188      fn checkpoint_snapshot(&mut self, snapshot_path: &Path, journal_path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
  189          let destructive_empty_checkpoint = self.messages.is_empty()
  190              && self.persist_state.messages_len > 0
  191              && snapshot_path.exists();
  192          if destructive_empty_checkpoint {
  193              self.guard_snapshot_shrink(snapshot_path, journal_path);
  194              bail!(
  195                  "refusing to replace non-empty persisted transcript for session {} with an empty checkpoint",
  196                  self.id
  197              );
  198          }
  199          self.guard_snapshot_shrink(snapshot_path, journal_path);
03
L1factobservability

03 · 观测覆盖结构化 lifecycle、实时 session metrics 与可选择遥测

源码事实

工具/MCP/Swarm/Session/Provider 路径记录带 session/message/tool id、latency、bytes、状态的结构化事件；独立 lock-free-ish session registry 维护 60 秒 token churn、turns 和 last activity。匿名 telemetry 默认启用，可用 JCODE_NO_TELEMETRY、DO_NOT_TRACK 或 marker 关闭；prompt/transcript 内容分享另行显式 opt-in。

白话解释

本地能看每个工人最近是否真在干活、花了多少 token、工具跑多久；发往服务端的匿名统计能关闭，而对话内容默认不发。

对自研 Harness 的含义

可观测维度很丰富；隐私评审需同时检查 usage telemetry、content-sharing 和 sponsor provenance 三条数据面。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 603–619
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 620–638
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

  643      /// Check if a tool output would overflow the context window and truncate if needed.
  644      /// Returns the (possibly truncated) output.
  645      async fn guard_context_overflow(&self, tool_name: &str, output: ToolOutput) -> ToolOutput {
  646          let compaction = self.compaction.read().await;
  647          let budget = compaction.token_budget();
  648          if budget == 0 {
  649              return output;
  650          }
  651
  652          let current_tokens = compaction.effective_token_count();
  653          let output_tokens = Self::estimate_tokens(&output.output);
  654
  655          // Check 1: Would adding this output push us over the safety threshold?
  656          let projected = current_tokens + output_tokens;
  657          let threshold_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::CONTEXT_GUARD_THRESHOLD) as usize;
  658
  659          // Check 2: Is this single output unreasonably large relative to budget?
  660          let single_max_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_FRACTION) as usize;
  661
  662          let needs_truncation = projected > threshold_tokens || output_tokens > single_max_tokens;
  663
  664          if !needs_truncation {
  665              return output;
  666          }
  667
  668          // Calculate how many tokens we can afford for this output
  669          let remaining = if current_tokens < threshold_tokens {
  670              threshold_tokens - current_tokens
  671          } else {
  672              // Already over threshold — allow a small amount for the error message
  673              budget / 50 // ~2% of budget for the truncation notice
  674          };
  675          let max_tokens = remaining.min(single_max_tokens);
  676
  677          // Convert token limit back to approximate character limit
  678          let max_chars = max_tokens * 4;
  679
  680          if output.output.len() <= max_chars {
  681              return output;
  682          }
  683
  684          crate::logging::info(&format!(
  685              "Context guard: truncating {} output from ~{}k to ~{}k tokens \
  686               (context: {}k/{}k, {:.0}% used)",
  687              tool_name,
  688              output_tokens / 1000,
  689              max_tokens / 1000,
  690              current_tokens / 1000,
  691              budget / 1000,
  692              (current_tokens as f32 / budget as f32) * 100.0,
  693          ));
  694
  695          // Truncate the output, keeping the beginning (usually most relevant)
  696          let truncated = if max_chars > 200 {
  697              // Keep beginning of output + truncation notice
  698              let kept = &output.output[..output.output.floor_char_boundary(max_chars - 150)];
  699              format!(
  700                  "{}\n\n⚠️ OUTPUT TRUNCATED: This tool output was {:.0}k tokens which would \
  701                   exceed the context window ({:.0}k/{}k tokens used, {}k budget). \
  702                   Only the first ~{:.0}k tokens are shown. Use more targeted queries \
  703                   (e.g., smaller line ranges, specific grep patterns) to get the content \
  704                   you need without exceeding context limits.",
  705                  kept,
  706                  output_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  707                  current_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  708                  budget / 1000,
  709                  budget / 1000,

crates/jcode-app-core/src/tool/mod.rs · 71fa60c4dc87当前源码 ↗
  603      ///
  604      /// It has one job: make the price legible and the retry obvious. The caller
  605      /// gets the exact cost, what would survive truncation, the cheap fixes, and
  606      /// the exact flag to pass if they really want the whole thing.
  607      fn oversized_output_refusal(
  608          output_tokens: usize,
  609          affordable_tokens: usize,
  610          current_tokens: usize,
  611          budget: usize,
  612      ) -> String {
  613          let percent_of_budget = if budget > 0 {
  614              (output_tokens as f32 / budget as f32) * 100.0
  615          } else {
  616              0.0
  617          };
  618          format!(
  619              "⚠️ OUTPUT WITHHELD: this result is ~{output}k tokens ({percent:.0}% of the \
  620               {budget}k context budget, of which {used}k is already used), so it was not \
  621               returned. Nothing was added to the context except this message.\n\n\
  622               Narrow the request first: add or tighten `path`, `glob`, or `type`, set \
  623               `max_files`/`max_regions`, use `paths_only` when you only need locations, or \
  624               read a specific line range. A targeted query is almost always the better \
  625               answer than a truncated dump.\n\n\
  626               If you genuinely need this output and accept the token cost, repeat the same \
  627               call with `\"accept_large_output\": true`. That returns the first ~{affordable}k \
  628               tokens and permanently spends them from this session's context.",
  629              output = output_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  630              percent = percent_of_budget,
  631              budget = budget / 1000,
  632              used = current_tokens / 1000,
  633              affordable = affordable_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  634          )
  635      }
  636
  637      /// Execute a tool by name
  638      pub async fn execute(&self, name: &str, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  639          // Mark this call in-flight for the whole execution so the missing
  640          // tool-output repair paths do not mistake a slow tool for an
  641          // interrupted one and inject a duplicate synthetic result. See
  642          // `tool::inflight`.
  643          let _in_flight = inflight::mark_tool_in_flight(&ctx.tool_call_id);
  644          let tools = self.tools.read().await;
  645          let resolved_name = Self::resolve_tool_name(name);
  646          if let Some(policy) = session_tool_policy(&ctx.session_id) {
  647              if let Some(allowed) = policy.allowed_tools.as_ref()
  648                  && !tool_name_is_allowed(allowed, resolved_name)
  649              {
  650                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is not allowed", resolved_name));
  651              }
  652              if tool_name_is_disabled(&policy.disabled_tools, resolved_name) {
  653                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is disabled", resolved_name));
  654              }
  655          }
  656          let tool = match tools.get(resolved_name) {
  657              Some(tool) => tool.clone(),
  658              None => {
  659                  // List available tools so the model can recover instead of
  660                  // spiraling through hallucinated names like "ToolSearch" (#104).
  661                  let mut available: Vec<&str> = tools.keys().map(|k| k.as_str()).collect();
  662                  available.sort_unstable();
  663                  let suggestions = Self::closest_tool_names(name, &available);
  664                  let mut msg = format!("Unknown tool: {name}.");
  665                  if !suggestions.is_empty() {
  666                      msg.push_str(&format!(" Did you mean: {}?", suggestions.join(", ")));
  667                  }
  668                  msg.push_str(&format!(" Available tools: {}.", available.join(", ")));
  669                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(msg));
  670              }
  671          };
  672
  673          // Drop the lock before executing
  674          drop(tools);
  675
  676          // User-configured pre_tool gate: external policy hook that can block
  677          // this call (exit 2). Skipped entirely when not configured.
  678          if crate::hooks::hook_configured("pre_tool") {
  679              let input_json = input.to_string();
  680              let working_dir = ctx
  681                  .working_dir
  682                  .as_ref()
  683                  .map(|dir| dir.display().to_string());
  684              let decision = crate::hooks::run_pre_tool_gate(
  685                  &ctx.session_id,
  686                  working_dir.as_deref(),
  687                  resolved_name,
  688                  &input_json,
  689              )
  690              .await;
  691              if let crate::hooks::GateDecision::Block { reason } = decision {
  692                  let mut fields =
  693                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("blocked", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  694                  fields.push(("block_reason".to_string(), reason.clone()));
  695                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  696                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
  697                      "Tool call blocked by pre_tool hook: {reason}"
  698                  ));
  699              }
  700          }
  701
  702          crate::logging::event_info(
  703              "TOOL_LIFECYCLE",
  704              Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("start", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx),
  705          );
  706
  707          let started_at = std::time::Instant::now();
  708          let result = tool.execute(input.clone(), ctx.clone()).await;
  709          let latency_ms = started_at.elapsed().as_millis().min(u128::from(u64::MAX)) as u64;
04
L3factobservability

04 · 关键故障边界有源码级回归测试，许可证为 MIT

源码事实

仓库对 compaction restore/hard threshold、mid-stream rollback、session journal corruption、MCP management、hook gate、Swarm prompt/assignment/report-back 和 tool schema 等关键路径有测试；LICENSE 为 MIT。

白话解释

不只是代码注释说“应该安全”：断流、损坏账本、深度协作契约等都有回归用例钉住。

对自研 Harness 的含义

成熟度高，但 1,198 个 Rust 文件的系统仍需 CI 测试矩阵覆盖多 Provider、三平台和远程 daemon。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

09
DIMENSION · COLLABORATION

协作与子 Agent

3 条可定位结论；每条按事实 → 白话 → 工程影响 → 源码摘录展开。

01
L1factcollaboration

01 · 轻量 swarm 先让协调者规划 2–4 个任务，再并发 fork Provider

源码事实

run_swarm_message 用当前 Agent 生成 JSON task plan，对每项创建 child Session、fork provider、继承模型/认证/cwd，移除 subagent/task/todo 等递归工具，并用 try_join_all 并发；最终把所有输出交给协调者综合。

白话解释

先拆成几张独立工单，让多个复制了同一模型线路的工人同时做，最后由原会话汇总。

对自研 Harness 的含义

简单易用；worker 共享 registry 与工作目录，文件级冲突仍需任务切分和外部 git/worktree 纪律。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 1528–1569
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 1570–1613
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

叙述来自历史证据账本 · 119301ac9d60 · 打开账本提交 ↗
crates/jcode-app-core/src/server/swarm.rs:1528–1613固定提交 ↗
 1528  pub(super) async fn run_swarm_task(
 1529      agent: Arc<Mutex<Agent>>,
 1530      description: &str,
 1531      subagent_type: &str,
 1532      prompt: &str,
 1533  ) -> Result<String> {
 1534      let started = Instant::now();
 1535      let (provider, registry, session_id, working_dir, coordinator_model, provider_key, route) = {
 1536          let agent = agent.lock().await;
 1537          (
 1538              agent.provider_fork(),
 1539              agent.registry(),
 1540              agent.session_id().to_string(),
 1541              agent.working_dir().map(PathBuf::from),
 1542              agent.provider_model(),
 1543              agent.session_provider_key(),
 1544              agent.session_route_api_method(),
 1545          )
 1546      };
 1547      let parent_session_id = session_id.clone();
 1548      let mut session = Session::create(
 1549          Some(session_id),
 1550          Some(format!("{} (@{} swarm)", description, subagent_type)),
 1551      );
 1552      let child_session_id = session.id.clone();
 1553      session.model = Some(coordinator_model);
 1554      // Inherit the coordinator's exact auth identity so the forked worker keeps
 1555      // the same provider/auth route (OAuth vs API, openai-compatible profile)
 1556      // instead of silently falling back to the config default on persistence.
 1557      session.provider_key = provider_key;
 1558      session.route_api_method = route;
 1559      if let Some(dir) = working_dir {
 1560          session.working_dir = Some(dir.display().to_string());
 1561      }
 1562      session.save()?;
 1563
 1564      log_swarm_lifecycle(
 1565          "task_start",
 1566          vec![
 1567              ("parent_session_id", parent_session_id.clone()),
 1568              ("child_session_id", child_session_id.clone()),
 1569              ("subagent_type", subagent_type.to_string()),
 1570              ("description_chars", description.chars().count().to_string()),
 1571              ("prompt_chars", prompt.chars().count().to_string()),
 1572          ],
 1573      );
 1574
 1575      let mut allowed: HashSet<String> = registry.tool_names().await.into_iter().collect();
 1576      for blocked in ["subagent", "task", "todo", "todowrite", "todoread"] {
 1577          allowed.remove(blocked);
 1578      }
 1579      crate::config::config()
 1580          .tools
 1581          .apply_to_allowed_set(&mut allowed);
 1582
 1583      let mut worker = Agent::new_with_session(provider, registry, session, Some(allowed));
 1584      match worker.run_once_capture(prompt).await {
 1585          Ok(output) => {
 1586              log_swarm_lifecycle(
 1587                  "task_done",
 1588                  vec![
 1589                      ("parent_session_id", parent_session_id),
 1590                      ("child_session_id", child_session_id),
 1591                      ("subagent_type", subagent_type.to_string()),
 1592                      ("output_chars", output.chars().count().to_string()),
 1593                      ("elapsed_ms", started.elapsed().as_millis().to_string()),
 1594                  ],
 1595              );
 1596              Ok(output)
 1597          }
 1598          Err(error) => {
 1599              crate::logging::event_warn(
 1600                  "SWARM_LIFECYCLE",
 1601                  vec![
 1602                      ("phase", "task_error".to_string()),
 1603                      ("parent_session_id", parent_session_id),
 1604                      ("child_session_id", child_session_id),
 1605                      ("subagent_type", subagent_type.to_string()),
 1606                      ("error", error.to_string()),
 1607                      ("elapsed_ms", started.elapsed().as_millis().to_string()),
 1608                  ],
 1609              );
 1610              Err(error)
 1611          }
 1612      }
 1613  }
 1614
 1615  pub(super) async fn run_swarm_message(agent: Arc<Mutex<Agent>>, message: &str) -> Result<String> {
 1616      let started = Instant::now();
 1617      log_swarm_lifecycle(
 1618          "message_start",
 1619          vec![("message_chars", message.chars().count().to_string())],
 1620      );
 1621      let working_dir = {
 1622          let agent = agent.lock().await;
 1623          agent.working_dir().map(|dir| dir.to_string())
 1624      };
 1625      let working_dir_hint = working_dir
 1626          .as_deref()
 1627          .map(|dir| format!("Working directory: {}\n", dir))
 1628          .unwrap_or_default();
 1629
 1630      let planner_prompt = format!(
 1631          "{working_dir_hint}You are a task planner. Break the request into 2-4 subtasks. \
 1632  Return ONLY a JSON array of objects with keys: description, prompt, subagent_type. \
 1633  No extra text.\n\nRequest:\n{message}"
 1634      );
 1635
 1636      let plan_text = {
 1637          let mut agent = agent.lock().await;
 1638          agent.run_once_capture(&planner_prompt).await?
 1639      };
 1640
 1641      let mut tasks = parse_swarm_tasks(&plan_text);
 1642      if tasks.is_empty() {
 1643          tasks.push(SwarmTaskSpec {
 1644              description: "Main task".to_string(),
 1645              prompt: message.to_string(),
 1646              subagent_type: Some("general".to_string()),
 1647          });
 1648      }
 1649      log_swarm_lifecycle(
 1650          "message_plan_done",
 1651          vec![
 1652              ("task_count", tasks.len().to_string()),
 1653              ("plan_chars", plan_text.chars().count().to_string()),
 1654          ],
 1655      );
 1656
 1657      let task_futures = tasks.iter().map(|task| {
 1658          let agent = agent.clone();
 1659          let working_dir_hint = working_dir_hint.clone();
 1660          let description = task.description.clone();
 1661          let prompt = format!("{working_dir_hint}{}", task.prompt);
 1662          let subagent_type = task
 1663              .subagent_type
 1664              .clone()
 1665              .unwrap_or_else(|| "general".to_string());
 1666          async move {
 1667              let output = run_swarm_task(agent, &description, &subagent_type, &prompt).await?;
 1668              Ok::<(String, String), anyhow::Error>((description, output))
 1669          }
 1670      });
 1671      let task_outputs = try_join_all(task_futures).await?;
 1672
 1673      let mut integration_prompt = String::new();
 1674      integration_prompt.push_str(
 1675          "You are the coordinator. Complete the original request using the subagent outputs below. ",
 1676      );
 1677      integration_prompt.push_str("Do not stop early; run any requested tests and fix failures.\n\n");
 1678      integration_prompt.push_str("Original request:\n");
 1679      integration_prompt.push_str(message);
 1680      integration_prompt.push_str("\n\nSubagent outputs:\n");
 1681      for (desc, output) in &task_outputs {
 1682          integration_prompt.push_str(&format!("\n--- {} ---\n{}\n", desc, output));
 1683      }
 1684      integration_prompt.push_str("\nNow complete the task.\n");
展开 28 行逐行翻译

02
L1factcollaboration

02 · deep swarm 把协作升级成可增长 DAG、强制 artifact 与审计 gate

源码事实

swarm-deep 动态指令要求 task_graph、depends_on、expand_node、complete_node typed artifact、critique/verify gate 与 inject_gap；worker 未 expand/complete 会重排队，gate 必须按 audited node id 覆盖范围并优先处理低置信 artifact。

白话解释

不是“多叫几个人聊天”，而是把任务画成图：每个节点要交证据包，审计员可以发现缺口后现场加新节点，图没验完就不能宣布完成。

对自研 Harness 的含义

非常接近研究/工程编排 Harness；复杂度、成本和并发写冲突风险也显著提高。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 75–82
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 83–91
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

   75  /// Reasoning-effort sentinel for the **deep task graph** mode: strongest model
   76  /// reasoning AND the comprehensive DAG-first swarm workflow (decompose into a
   77  /// validated task graph, critique/verify gates, typed artifact handoffs). Sits
   78  /// one rung above [`SWARM_EFFORT`] on the effort ladder: `... xhigh`, `swarm`
   79  /// (light fan-out), `swarm-deep` (deep task graph). Providers translate this to
   80  /// their strongest real effort, while the UI/session keep the literal marker so
   81  /// the agent knows to inject [`SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT_DIRECTIVE`].
   82  pub const SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT: &str = "swarm-deep";
   83
   84  /// System-prompt directive injected when the active reasoning effort is
   85  /// [`SWARM_EFFORT`]. Instructs the agent to lean on the swarm tooling.
   86  pub const SWARM_EFFORT_DIRECTIVE: &str = "# Swarm Effort\n\nYou are running at the maximum reasoning effort with swarm orchestration enabled. For any non-trivial task, decompose the work and use the `swarm` tool to spawn and coordinate parallel agents (spawn workers with concrete prompts, assign tasks, and collect their reports) instead of doing everything yourself in one thread. Prefer parallelizing independent subtasks across swarm members, and use a coordinator/plan when the work has multiple stages. Only skip the swarm for trivial, single-step requests.";
   87
   88  /// System-prompt directive injected when the active reasoning effort is
   89  /// [`SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT`]. Instructs the agent to run the comprehensive DAG-first
   90  /// task-graph workflow.
   91  pub const SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT_DIRECTIVE: &str = "# Deep Task Graph\n\nYou are running at maximum reasoning effort with the deep task-graph swarm workflow. Treat the task DAG as the primary object, not ad hoc agent chat. Workflow:\n\n1. Seed a graph with `swarm task_graph` using `mode: \"deep\"`: lay out nodes (kind explore|implement|verify|fix|synthesize) and `depends_on` edges instead of answering directly. (At this effort the server already defaults the plan to deep, but pass `mode: \"deep\"` explicitly anyway.) The engine auto-inserts a plan-wide root gate over your seed: the plan cannot finish until a final adversarial audit passes, and that audit can inject new top-level work.\n2. For any node that is too big, `swarm expand_node` to decompose it into a child sub-DAG (you become its planner/integrator). In deep mode a critique/verify gate is auto-inserted before a composite node can close. The graph is EXPECTED to outgrow its seed, often by several times: growth (expansions and gate-injected gaps) is the system working, not scope creep. plan_status reports seeded-vs-grown counts.\n3. Finish each node with `swarm complete_node` and a typed artifact: `findings`, `evidence` (file:line / commit refs), `validation`, `open_questions`, a required `confidence` (low|medium|high; report low honestly, it routes follow-up work to shore up that scope), and an honest `what_i_did_not_check`. Downstream nodes are hydrated with these artifacts automatically. There is no other way to close a deep node: a turn ending without expand_node/complete_node re-queues the node to a fresh worker and fails it on repeat.\n4. When a critique/verify gate finds gaps or failures, use `swarm inject_gap` to add new nodes; the parent cannot close until they drain. A passing gate artifact must account for EVERY node it audited by id (the server rejects rubber stamps), and cannot pass over a low-confidence sibling without addressing it explicitly, so treat low-confidence siblings as priority probe targets.\n5. Use `swarm run_plan` to drive the graph to completion. It returns immediately and drives the plan as a background task (progress card + wake on completion), so keep working or answer the user while it runs; check `swarm plan_status` or `bg` for progress. Deep mode fans out wide automatically (many workers run in parallel, bounded only by the swarm member cap), so prefer decomposing into MANY independent sibling nodes rather than a few serial ones: keep the ready set wide so run_plan can dispatch lots of agents at once. Only add `depends_on` edges for real data dependencies.\n\nComprehensiveness is structural: prefer decomposition + gates over a single thorough answer, so it is very unlikely any nook or cranny is missed.";
   92
   93  /// Returns true when `effort` is either swarm sentinel (light or deep),
   94  /// case-insensitive. Used by providers to map to the strongest real effort.
   95  pub fn is_swarm_effort(effort: &str) -> bool {
   96      let trimmed = effort.trim();
   97      trimmed.eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_EFFORT) || trimmed.eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT)
   98  }
   99
  100  /// Returns true when `effort` is specifically the deep task-graph sentinel.
  101  pub fn is_deep_swarm_effort(effort: &str) -> bool {
  102      effort.trim().eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT)
  103  }
  104
  105  /// The user-facing "general effort" ladder is one list, but each rung is one of
  106  /// two internal kinds: a plain reasoning level (mapped straight to the provider
  107  /// wire effort) or a swarm orchestration mode (which also pins reasoning to the
  108  /// model's max). [`EffortKind`] is the single classifier all consumers use so the
  109  /// UI, providers, and scheduler never disagree about what a rung means.
  110  #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
  111  pub enum EffortKind {
  112      /// A plain reasoning level (none/low/medium/high/xhigh/max).
  113      Reasoning,
  114      /// Light swarm mode: max reasoning + parallel fan-out.
  115      SwarmLight,
  116      /// Deep swarm mode: max reasoning + DAG-first task graph.
  117      SwarmDeep,
  118  }
  119
  120  impl EffortKind {
  121      /// True when this rung is a swarm orchestration mode rather than a plain
  122      /// reasoning level. Such rungs must not be treated as per-model effort
  123      /// variants (e.g. they should not generate `model (effort)` picker rows).
  124      pub fn is_swarm_mode(self) -> bool {
  125          matches!(self, EffortKind::SwarmLight | EffortKind::SwarmDeep)
  126      }
  127  }
  128
  129  /// Classify a general-effort rung string into its [`EffortKind`].
  130  pub fn classify_effort(effort: &str) -> EffortKind {
  131      let trimmed = effort.trim();
  132      if trimmed.eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT) {
  133          EffortKind::SwarmDeep
  134      } else if trimmed.eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_EFFORT) {
  135          EffortKind::SwarmLight
  136      } else {
  137          EffortKind::Reasoning
  138      }
  139  }
  140
  141  /// True when an effort rung is a swarm orchestration mode (light or deep) rather
  142  /// than a plain reasoning level. Convenience wrapper over [`classify_effort`].
  143  pub fn is_swarm_mode_effort(effort: &str) -> bool {
  144      classify_effort(effort).is_swarm_mode()
  145  }
  146
  147  /// Append the appropriate swarm directive to a split prompt's dynamic part when
  148  /// the active reasoning effort is a swarm sentinel. The deep sentinel injects the
  149  /// DAG-first task-graph directive; the light sentinel injects the fan-out
  150  /// directive. No-op otherwise.
  151  pub fn append_swarm_effort_directive(split: &mut SplitSystemPrompt, effort: Option<&str>) {
  152      let directive = match effort {
  153          Some(effort) if is_deep_swarm_effort(effort) => SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT_DIRECTIVE,
  154          Some(effort) if is_swarm_effort(effort) => SWARM_EFFORT_DIRECTIVE,
  155          _ => return,
  156      };
  157      if !split.dynamic_part.is_empty() {
  158          split.dynamic_part.push_str("\n\n");
  159      }
  160      split.dynamic_part.push_str(directive);
  161  }

crates/jcode-base/src/prompt.rs · 71fa60c4dc87当前源码 ↗
   75  /// project `./.jcode/swarm-prompt.md`, then global `~/.jcode/swarm-prompt.md`,
   76  /// then the built-in [`DEFAULT_SWARM_PROMPT`].
   77  pub fn load_swarm_prompt(working_dir: Option<&Path>) -> String {
   78      let project_dir = working_dir.unwrap_or(Path::new("."));
   79      let candidates = [
   80          Some(project_dir.join(".jcode").join("swarm-prompt.md")),
   81          crate::storage::jcode_dir()
   82              .ok()
   83              .map(|dir| dir.join("swarm-prompt.md")),
   84      ];
   85      for path in candidates.into_iter().flatten() {
   86          if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
   87              let trimmed = content.trim();
   88              if !trimmed.is_empty() {
   89                  return trimmed.to_string();
   90              }
   91          }
   92      }
   93      DEFAULT_SWARM_PROMPT.trim().to_string()
   94  }

  134  /// two internal kinds: a plain reasoning level (mapped straight to the provider
  135  /// wire effort) or a swarm orchestration mode (which also pins reasoning to the
  136  /// model's max). [`EffortKind`] is the single classifier all consumers use so the
  137  /// UI, providers, and scheduler never disagree about what a rung means.
  138  #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
  139  pub enum EffortKind {
  140      /// A plain reasoning level (none/low/medium/high/xhigh/max).
  141      Reasoning,
  142      /// Light swarm mode: max reasoning + parallel fan-out.
  143      SwarmLight,
  144      /// Deep swarm mode: max reasoning + DAG-first task graph.
  145      SwarmDeep,
  146  }
  147
  148  impl EffortKind {
  149      /// True when this rung is a swarm orchestration mode rather than a plain
  150      /// reasoning level. Such rungs must not be treated as per-model effort
  151      /// variants (e.g. they should not generate `model (effort)` picker rows).
  152      pub fn is_swarm_mode(self) -> bool {
  153          matches!(self, EffortKind::SwarmLight | EffortKind::SwarmDeep)
  154      }
  155  }
  156
  157  /// Classify a general-effort rung string into its [`EffortKind`].
  158  pub fn classify_effort(effort: &str) -> EffortKind {
  159      let trimmed = effort.trim();
  160      if trimmed.eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_DEEP_EFFORT) {
  161          EffortKind::SwarmDeep
  162      } else if trimmed.eq_ignore_ascii_case(SWARM_EFFORT) {
03
L1factcollaboration

03 · Swarm 有持久成员树、频道、heartbeat 和死亡任务回收

源码事实

SwarmMemberRecord 保存 parent/report-back、角色、状态、working_dir 和 report；ChannelIndex 双向索引订阅。worker 死亡时非终态任务按 reclaim cap 重新排队，超上限转 failed，并持久化/broadcast/通知协调者。

白话解释

每个工人有户口、上级、频道和心跳；工人挂了，手里的活不会静默卡死，会有限次数重新派发，反复致命才明确失败。

对自研 Harness 的含义

协作是耐久调度系统而非内存 future；需要持续治理 member cap、staleness 和 replay idempotency。

逐段阅读提示
  1. 213–232
    入口、配置和状态初始化

    先确认谁拿到输入、读取了哪些配置、创建了哪些状态。

  2. 233–253
    分支、副作用和回执

    再追踪条件分支、外部调用、事件写入和失败出口；不要只看函数名。

  3. after
    回到调用者

    最后沿着调用者和下一条事件继续读，确认这段代码的结果如何被消费。

  213  /// Durable, persistable portion of a swarm member.
  214  #[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]
  215  pub struct SwarmMemberRecord {
